Quantum geometric tensors from sub-bundle geometry
Marius A. Oancea, Thomas B. Mieling, Giandomenico Palumbo

Abstract
The geometric properties of quantum states are crucial for understanding many physical phenomena in quantum mechanics, condensed matter physics, and optics. The central object describing these properties is the quantum geometric tensor, which unifies the Berry curvature and the quantum metric. In this work, we use the differential-geometric framework of vector bundles to analyze the properties of parameter-dependent quantum states and generalize the quantum geometric tensor to this setting. This construction is based on a general connection on a Hermitian vector bundle, which defines a notion of quantum state transport in parameter space, and a sub-bundle projector, which constrains the set of accessible quantum states. We show that the sub-bundle geometry is similar to that of submanifolds in Riemannian geometry and is described by generalized Gauss–Codazzi–Mainardi equations. This leads to a novel definition of the quantum geometric tensor that contains an additional curvature contribution. To illustrate our results, we describe the sub-bundle geometry arising in the semiclassical treatment of Dirac fields propagating in curved spacetime and show how the quantum geometric tensor, with its additional curvature contributions, is obtained in this case. As a concrete example, we consider Dirac fermions confined to a hyperbolic plane and demonstrate how spatial curvature influences the quantum geometry. This work sets the stage for further exploration of quantum systems in curved geometries, with applications in both high-energy physics and condensed matter systems.



1
Introduction

Geometric phases are fundamental concepts in quantum mechanical systems, describing phase differences acquired by the wave functions of quantum states that are induced by the non-trivial geometry of the parameter space in which the systems evolve
[
1
,
2
]
. Importantly, because of their geometric origin
[
3
,
2
]
, they are independent of the specific dynamical evolution of the quantum states.

Quantum geometry has emerged as a central concept in modern condensed matter physics
[
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
]
, providing insights into various quantum phenomena through the quantum geometric tensor. This complex rank-2 tensor is a gauge-invariant physical quantity that has already been measured in several experimental setups
[
9
,
10
,
11
,
12
]
.
Moreover, this tensor unifies the Berry curvature
[
1
,
3
]
and the quantum metric
[
13
]
that have been used to characterize the topology and geometry of quantum states in parameter spaces
[
14
,
15
,
16
,
17
]
, momentum spaces
[
18
,
19
,
20
,
21
,
22
,
23
,
24
,
25
]
, and configuration spaces
[
26
,
27
]
. Recent developments have significantly broadened the scope of quantum geometry, extending its range of applicability to include degenerate quantum states
[
28
,
29
,
30
,
31
,
32
,
33
,
34
,
35
,
36
]
, flat-band superconductors
[
37
,
38
,
39
,
40
]
, tensor monopoles
[
41
,
42
,
43
]
, Euler insulators
[
44
,
45
,
46
]
, non-Hermitian physics
[
47
,
48
,
49
,
50
,
51
]
, out-of-equilibrium systems
[
52
,
53
,
54
,
55
]
, interacting phases
[
56
,
57
,
58
,
59
,
60
,
61
,
62
,
63
,
64
,
65
,
66
,
67
,
68
]
, quantum systems with spin-topology
[
69
]
, geometric semimetals
[
70
]
, finite-temperature mixed states
[
71
,
72
]
, and more. These studies have underscored the importance of generalizing the notion of quantum metrics to accommodate more intricate scenarios encountered in contemporary research.

In this paper, we present a general construction of the quantum geometric tensor within the differential geometric framework of vector bundles
[
73
,
74
]
. This can be related to the usual descriptions of quantum geometry in condensed matter physics by identifying the base space of the vector bundle with the parameter space of a quantum system and the fibers of the vector bundle with finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces, thus describing the parameter-dependent space of quantum states. To cover as many physical scenarios as possible, we only introduce a minimum amount of geometric structure, and all our calculations are presented in a coordinate-independent way. More concretely, we assume the complex vector bundles to be equipped with a Hermitian pseudo-metric, a compatible connection, and an orthogonal projector, which defines an orthogonal decomposition of the bundle. The sub-bundle geometry induced by the projector and the connection represents the quantum geometry usually studied in condensed matter physics and quantum mechanics
[
13
,
28
,
32
]
.
This geometric framework allows for natural definitions of the quantum geometric tensor, the Berry connection, and the quantum metric, which generalize previous constructions in several ways.

From a mathematical perspective, the induced sub-bundle geometry is very similar to the classical problem in Riemannian geometry regarding the geometry of hypersurfaces
[
75
]
, or more generally, the geometry of submanifolds
[
76
,
77
]
. We show that the sub-bundle geometry is described by a generalized form of the Gauss–Codazzi–Mainardi equations (similar generalizations are also given in Refs.
[
73
,
74
]
, although only for the case of holomorphic vector bundles over complex manifolds). The main geometric objects that describe the sub-bundle geometry are the projected connection, which is essentially the Berry connection, the first fundamental form represented by the fiber metric, and the second fundamental form or shape operator
[
78
, p. 235]
. The quantum geometric tensor then arises as a derived object that is obtained by contracting the shape operator with its adjoint via the first fundamental form, which can be seen as a generalization of the third fundamental form of classical differential geometry
[
75
, p. 62]
.

The first mathematical definition of the quantum metric was given in Ref.
[
13
]
as a measure of the distance between neighboring quantum states that depend on a set of parameters. The quantum geometric tensor, which unifies the quantum metric and the Berry curvature, was later introduced by Berry
[
79
]
(see also Ref.
[
2
]
for a textbook treatment). More general definitions of the quantum geometric tensor in Hermitian systems, covering the non-Abelian case and emphasizing the role of the projector, were given later in Refs.
[
28
,
32
,
33
]
. Compared with these previous works, our results provide a more general definition of the quantum geometric tensor in the following ways. First, some of the previous works assume that the parameter space is
\mathbb{R}^{N}
, while in our case it is represented by a generic manifold. Second, and most importantly, in all previous papers a flat connection was assumed when taking derivatives of quantum states, and the Berry connection was defined by projecting the flat connection onto the relevant subspace. Working with a general connection on the complex vector bundle instead, we obtain an additional curvature contribution to the quantum geometric tensor. This shows that the common statement that “the antisymmetric part of the quantum geometric tensor gives the Berry curvature” is only true when the Berry connection is the projection of a flat connection. Similarly to Ref.
[
28
]
, our treatment of the quantum geometric tensor covers both the Abelian and non-Abelian cases, since we do not make any particular assumptions about the rank of the projector, besides being constant. Another general feature of our approach is that we work with a general Hermitian pseudo-metric which is allowed to vary between different points in the base manifold.

A more general notion of the quantum geometric tensor, especially by allowing the parameter space to be a manifold and working with a general connection, is fundamental for the proper description of certain physical scenarios. For example, the Berry curvature is essential for the description of semiclassical wave packet dynamics
[
80
,
81
]
, but general-relativistic extensions of these results require working on manifolds and using non-flat connections. Such generalized forms of Berry curvature have been shown, for instance, to determine gravitational spin Hall effects
[
82
]
, which govern the spin-dependent semiclassical propagation of electromagnetic
[
83
,
84
,
85
,
86
]
, linearized gravitational
[
87
,
88
,
89
,
90
]
, and Dirac
[
91
,
92
]
wave packets in curved spacetime. As an application of our geometric framework, we show how the quantum geometric tensor can be obtained for semiclassical Dirac fields on general spacetimes. In this way, we recover the Berry curvature introduced in Ref.
[
92
]
, and we also obtain the corresponding quantum metric, which naturally encodes unique aspects of quantum states coupled to a non-trivial spacetime geometry. In fact, in addition to generalizing the static phase-space Berry curvature discussed in Refs.
[
80
,
81
,
93
]
and the more recent time-dependent quantum geometric tensor presented in Refs.
[
53
,
94
,
95
]
, our formulation of the non-Abelian quantum metric induced by a spacetime metric is completely novel.

This paper is structured as follows.
Section

2
presents the general differential geometric setup. We review the standard definitions of connections and curvature in complex vector bundles and introduce the orthogonal projectors. In
Section

3
, we present our general theory for the quantum geometric tensor, based on the central role of projected covariant derivatives and shape operators. Finally, in
Section

4
, we discuss some applications of our results in the context of Dirac fermions on curved spacetime. In particular, to provide a more direct application of our framework in condensed matter physics, we use our generalized quantum metric to study Dirac fermions on the hyperbolic plane, which are relevant in the context of topological phases on hyperbolic lattices
[
96
,
97
,
98
,
99
,
100
,
101
]
in the low-energy regime. Our results are not only relevant for understanding and investigating the quantum geometry of hyperbolic topological matter, but provide a robust theoretical foundation for future explorations of quantum systems coupled to static and dynamical background geometries across solid-state and synthetic matter systems of any dimension
[
102
,
103
,
104
,
105
,
106
,
107
,
108
,
109
,
110
,
111
,
112
,
113
,
114
]
.

2
Differential geometric setup

Our main goal is the description of parameter-dependent quantum systems and their evolution under slow changes in parameter space.
This means that for each parameter
\lambda
in a parameter space
M
, there is a finite-dimensional space of states
\mathscr{H}(\lambda)
that is isomorphic (though not necessarily naturally) to
\mathbb{C}^{n}
, with the dimension
n
being the same for all values of
\lambda
.
Formally, such a structure is a vector bundle
E
over
M
with typical fiber
\mathbb{C}^{n}
.
In this picture, a constraint (that may depend on the parameters
\lambda
) implies that not all states in
E
are accessible. Instead, if the number of constraints is constant for all
\lambda
, the accessible states form a sub-bundle
E^{\prime}
of
E
(with typical fiber
\mathbb{C}^{m}
where
m<n
).
Such descriptions apply, for example, to condensed matter systems, where electrons in periodic lattices are constrained to certain energy bands
[
115
]
, or to Wentzel–Kramers–Brillouin (WKB)-type approximations, where the amplitude is constrained to the eigenspace of the principal symbol of the corresponding differential operator
[
116
,
117
,
92
]
.

The theory developed here requires further mathematical structures on the bundle
E
, namely a (potentially indefinite) fiber metric and a compatible connection.
If all spaces
\mathscr{H}(\lambda)
are Hilbert spaces, then the various inner products define a fiber metric (provided that they vary continuously with
\lambda
). However, we do not restrict to such positive-definite products, but also allow for an indefinite product as arising in the case of Dirac
4
-spinors or for pseudo-Hermitian quantum systems
[
118
,
119
]
, for example.
In either case, in order to compare states in different state spaces, a connection on
E
is required.

The aim of this section is to make this heuristic picture mathematically precise. More details on the differential geometry of complex vector bundles can be found in Refs.
[
73
,
74
]
.

2.1
Bundles, connections, and curvature

Let
M
be a real
C^{\infty}
manifold of dimension
N<\infty
and denote by
\mathscr{C}_{\mathbb{R}}(M)
and
\mathscr{C}_{\mathbb{C}}(M)
the rings of real and complex scalar functions on
M
, respectively, and by
\mathfrak{X}(M)
the module of real vector fields on
M
(all assumed to be smooth).
Depending on the physical setting, the manifold
M
can represent different objects, such as spacetime, a Lagrangian submanifold (in the context of WKB-type semiclassical treatments
[
117
,
92
]
), momentum space (in condensed matter scenarios
[
18
,
19
,
20
,
21
,
22
,
23
,
24
,
25
]
), or some general parameter space
[
13
,
15
,
16
,
17
,
28
,
32
]
. However, in most previous treatments of the quantum geometric tensor, the parameter space was assumed to be
\mathbb{R}^{N}
.

We consider a complex vector bundle
(E,M,\pi_{E},\mathbb{C}^{n})
with base space
M
, projection
{\pi_{E}:E\to M}
, and typical fiber
\mathbb{C}^{n}
, but all the arguments below can be carried over to the real setting without significant modification.
In either case, the space of sections of
E
will be denoted by
\Gamma(E)
.

A non-degenerate Hermitian form on
E
is a map
h:\Gamma(E)\times\Gamma(E)\to\mathscr{C}_{\mathbb{C}}(M)
satisfying

\displaystyle h(\Psi,\cdot)=0\Leftrightarrow\Psi=0\,,

(2.1a)

\displaystyle h(\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2})=\overline{h(\Psi_{2},\Psi_{1})}\,,

(2.1b)

\displaystyle h(\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2}+f\Psi_{3})=h(\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2})+fh(\Psi_{1},\Psi_{3})\,,

(2.1c)

where
\Psi,\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2},\Psi_{3}\in\Gamma(E)
are arbitrary sections,
f\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathbb{C}}(M)
is any complex scalar field, and overlines denote complex conjugation. With this additional structure, the bundles are referred to as Hermitian vector bundles
[
73
,
74
]
.
Note that this does not entail that
h
is a Hermitian
metric
on the fibers of
E
since positive-definiteness is not assumed—instead
h
can be regarded as a Hermitian pseudo-metric on the fibers.
The action of the form
h
can also be translated into the Dirac notation as
h(\Phi,\Psi)=\braket{\Phi\mid\Psi}
.

A covariant derivative (also referred to as a connection) on
E
is a map

\displaystyle\nabla:\mathfrak{X}(M)\times\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(E)

\displaystyle(X,\Phi)\mapsto\nabla_{X}\Phi

(2.2)

such that for all vector fields
X,X_{1},X_{2}\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
, sections
\Phi,\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}\in\Gamma(E)
, real-valued scalar fields
\lambda\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathbb{R}}(M)
and complex scalar fields
f\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathbb{C}}(M)
one has

\displaystyle\nabla_{X_{1}+\lambda X_{2}}\Phi

\displaystyle=\nabla_{X_{1}}\Phi+\lambda\nabla_{X_{2}}\Phi\,,

(2.3a)

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}(\Phi_{1}+f\,\Phi_{2})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{X}\Phi_{1}+f\nabla_{X}\Phi_{2}+(Xf)\Phi_{2}\,,

(2.3b)

where
Xf
denotes the directional derivative of
f
along
X
.
This connection on
E
naturally induces further connections on
E^{\prime}
, the dual bundle of
E
, as well as their tensor products. The connection
\nabla
is said to be compatible with the Hermitian form
h
if, for all
X\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
and
\Phi,\Psi\in\Gamma(E)
, one has

\displaystyle X[h(\Phi,\Psi)]

\displaystyle=h(\nabla_{X}\Phi,\Psi)+h(\Phi,\nabla_{X}\Psi)\,.

(2.4)

In the following, this compatibility condition will be assumed throughout.

The curvature of the covariant derivative
\nabla
is the unique multilinear map

\displaystyle\Omega:\mathfrak{X}(M)\times\mathfrak{X}(M)\times\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(E)

\displaystyle(X,Y,\Phi)\mapsto\Omega(X,Y)\Phi\,,

(2.5)

that satisfies

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}\nabla_{Y}\Phi-\nabla_{Y}\nabla_{X}\Phi-\nabla_{[X,Y]}\Phi=\Omega(X,Y)\Phi\,,

(2.6)

where
[X,Y]
denotes the Lie bracket of
X
and
Y
.
The curvature satisfies
\Omega(X,Y)=-\Omega(Y,X)
and (due to the compatibility of
\nabla
with
h
)

\displaystyle h[\Omega(X,Y)\Phi,\Psi]=-h[\Phi,\Omega(X,Y)\Psi]\,.

(2.7)

We emphasize that this geometric setup is more general than that of previous treatments of the quantum geometric tensor, where the parameter space was generally assumed to be
\mathbb{R}^{N}
and a flat connection
\nabla=\partial
with
\Omega=0
was used.
Moreover, while our analysis allows for general
\nabla
, the specific connection in concrete applications cannot be chosen arbitrarily, but is determined by the physical problem.
For example, the relevant connection entering the semiclassical dynamics of spinors in curved spacetimes or hyperbolic Dirac materials is determined by the Levi-Civita connection and the spin structure of the spacetime manifold, as discussed in
Section

4
below.

2.2
Frames and index notation

A local frame of
E
over an open subset
U\subseteq M
consists of an ordered collection of local sections
(s_{i})=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{n})
with
s_{i}\in\Gamma(E|_{U})
such that
\{s_{1}(x),s_{2}(x),\ldots,s_{n}(x)\}
forms a basis of the fiber
E_{x}
for all
x\in U
.
Given such a local frame, the associated coframe
(\sigma^{i})
consists, at each point, of the dual basis vectors that satisfy
\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}})=\mathchoice{\delta^{{{i}}}_{{{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}}}_{{{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}}}_{{{j}}}}{\delta^{{{i}}}_{{{j}}}}
.
Relative to a frame
(s_{i})
,
h
is described by the fields

\displaystyle\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}=h(s_{i},s_{j})\,,

(2.8)

which assign to each point
x\in U
a non-degenerate Hermitian matrix
(\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}})
.
The connection
\nabla
, on the other hand, is described by a collection of one-forms acting on a vector field
X
as

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\omega^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\omega^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\omega^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{\omega^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}(X)=\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\nabla_{X}\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}})\,.

(2.9)

Since

is assumed to be non-degenerate,

(2.13)

where
\mathrm{d}
and
\wedge
denote exterior derivatives and exterior products, respectively.
In particular, relative to a coordinate frame, the above formula reduces to

(2.14)

2.3
Projections and sub-bundles

If
E
is to be interpreted as an unconstrained space of states, imposing a set of linear constraints can be formulated in terms of a projector
P
.
This is an
h
-compatible bundle-endomorphism of
E
satisfying

\displaystyle P^{2}

\displaystyle=P\,,

\displaystyle h(\Phi,P\Psi)=h(P\Phi,\Psi)\,.

(2.15)

The intuition behind this notion is that the “space of constrained states”
E_{\parallel}=\image P
contains all states satisfying a system of linear equations
P\Phi=\Phi
, while the complement
E_{\perp}=\kernel P
contains all remaining states satisfying
P\Psi=0
. The sub-bundles
E_{\parallel}=\image P
and
E_{\perp}=\kernel P
then yield the orthogonal decomposition

\displaystyle E=E_{\parallel}\oplus E_{\perp}\,.

(2.16)

Given a projector
P
, it is beneficial to use adapted frames of the form
(\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}})=(\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}},\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}})
, where the sections
\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}
and
\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}
span the fibers of
E_{\parallel}
and
E_{\perp}
, respectively.
The corresponding dual frame
(\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}})=(\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}})
then satisfies

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}})

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\delta^{{{A}}}_{{{B}}}}{\delta^{{{A}}}_{{{B}}}}{\delta^{{{A}}}_{{{B}}}}{\delta^{{{A}}}_{{{B}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{A}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{I}}}})

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{B}}}})

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1884pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27742pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{J}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1884pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27742pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{J}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1884pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27742pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{J}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.08472pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1884pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.27742pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{J}}}})

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\delta^{{{I}}}_{{{J}}}}{\delta^{{{I}}}_{{{J}}}}{\delta^{{{I}}}_{{{J}}}}{\delta^{{{I}}}_{{{J}}}}\,.

(2.17)

The mathematical picture of these projectors and the associated sub-bundle geometry is realized, for example, in condensed matter physics when electrons are constrained to certain energy bands
[
115
]
. Another example of such a decomposition arises when considering WKB-type approximations for multicomponent fields, where the WKB amplitude is constrained to an eigenspace of the principal symbol of the corresponding differential operator
[
116
,
117
,
92
]
. The rank of
P
is assumed to be constant on the parameter manifold
M
, and similarly to the analysis of Ref.
[
28
]
this can lead to an Abelian (
\rank P=1
) or non-Abelian (
1<\rank P<n
) quantum geometric tensor.

The projector
P
is compatible with the Hermitian form
h
, but need not be compatible with the covariant derivative
\nabla
.
The consequences of
\nabla_{X}P\neq 0
, described in detail in
Section

3
below, are the main source of the interesting properties associated with Berry connections, quantum geometric tensors, and quantum geometry in general.

3
Berry connection, shape operators, and derived quantities

In this section, we present our main mathematical results regarding the quantum geometry, or in other words the sub-bundle geometry, determined by the covariant derivative
\nabla
and the projector
P
.
In particular, we identify the shape operators as central geometric quantities as they fully determine the quantum geometric tensor, and provide a geometric decomposition of the quantum geometric tensor into
three
parts, namely the quantum metric, the Berry curvature, and the bundle curvature (which was absent in previous work on the subject).

3.1
Berry connection and shape operators

We start here by introducing the main geometric objects that describe the sub-bundle geometry. These are the projected covariant derivatives, which are directly related to the Berry connection, and the shape operators or the second fundamental forms, which describe the difference between
\nabla
and the projected connections via the Weingarten equations
[
76
, p. 35]
.

3.1.1
Projected covariant derivatives

In general, the covariant derivative
\nabla
is not compatible with the projection
P
. This means that if
\Phi_{\parallel}
and
\Psi_{\perp}
are sections of
E
taking values in
E_{\parallel}
and
E_{\perp}
, respectively, then
\nabla_{X}\Phi_{\parallel}
and
\nabla_{X}\Psi_{\perp}
are not guaranteed to take values in
E_{\parallel}
and
E_{\perp}
, respectively.
This can be seen as follows. Writing
P^{\prime}=I-P
for the projection onto
E_{\perp}
, one has

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}\Phi_{\parallel}

\displaystyle=P\nabla_{X}\Phi_{\parallel}+P^{\prime}\nabla_{X}\Phi_{\parallel}\,,

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}\Psi_{\perp}

\displaystyle=P^{\prime}\nabla_{X}\Psi_{\perp}+P\nabla_{X}\Psi_{\perp}\,.

(3.1)

The second terms in both equations, describing the contributions in the respective complementary bundles, can be expressed in terms of
\nabla_{X}P
, namely

\displaystyle P^{\prime}\nabla_{X}\Phi_{\parallel}

\displaystyle=+(\nabla_{X}P)\Phi_{\parallel}\,,

\displaystyle P\nabla_{X}\Psi_{\perp}

\displaystyle=-(\nabla_{X}P)\Psi_{\perp}\,.

(3.2)

This shows that taking
\nabla
-derivatives of sections of
E_{\perp}
or
E_{\parallel}
leads out of these bundles whenever
\nabla_{X}P\neq 0
.
This can be resolved by defining the projected connections:

\displaystyle D{{}_{X}}\Phi_{\parallel}

\displaystyle=P(\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{X}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{X}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{X}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{X}}}}\Phi_{\parallel})\,,

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{X}\Psi_{\perp}

\displaystyle=P^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{X}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{X}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{X}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{X}}}}\Psi_{\parallel})\,.

(3.3)

Using the methods described in
Section

2.2
, we can compute the connection coefficients and curvature tensors for these two covariant derivatives. In particular, the Berry connection coefficients are
[

]

.

3.1.2
Shape operators

Equation

3.2
shows that the differences between
\nabla
and the projected connections
D
and
D^{\prime}
can be understood in terms of the shape operators

\displaystyle S:\Gamma(E_{\parallel})\times\mathfrak{X}(M)

\displaystyle\to\Gamma(E_{\perp})\,,

\displaystyle S(\Phi_{\parallel},X)

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{X}P)\Phi_{\parallel}\,,

(3.6a)

\displaystyle S^{\dagger}:\Gamma(E_{\perp})\times\mathfrak{X}(M)

\displaystyle\to\Gamma(E_{\parallel})\,,

\displaystyle S^{\dagger}(\Phi_{\perp},X)

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{X}P)\Phi_{\perp}\,.

(3.6b)

The notation is justified by the fact that the operators
S_{X}\equiv S(\cdot,X)
and
S^{\dagger}_{X}\equiv S^{\dagger}(\cdot,X)
are adjoint in the sense that

h[\Phi_{\perp},S^{\phantom{\dagger}}_{X}\Psi_{\parallel}]=h[S^{\dagger}_{X}\Phi_{\perp},\Psi_{\parallel}]
,
which can be shown by differentiating
h(\Phi_{\perp},\Psi_{\parallel})=0
.
The expression
h[\Phi_{\perp},S^{\phantom{\dagger}}_{X}\Psi_{\parallel}]
arising here is multilinear in the arguments
\Phi_{\perp}
,
\Psi_{\parallel}
and
X
, and is known as the second fundamental form:

\displaystyle\underline{S}:\Gamma(E_{\perp})\times\Gamma(E_{\parallel})\times\mathfrak{X}(M)\to\mathscr{C}_{\mathbb{C}}\,,

\displaystyle\underline{S}(\Phi_{\perp},\Psi_{\parallel};X)=h[\Phi_{\perp},S_{X}\Psi_{\perp}]\,.

(3.7)

Combining
Eqs.

3.1
and
3.2
leads to the Weingarten equations

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}\Phi_{\parallel}

\displaystyle=D_{X}\Phi_{\parallel}+S^{\phantom{\dagger}}_{X}(\Phi_{\parallel})\,,

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}\Psi_{\perp}

\displaystyle=D^{\prime}_{X}\Psi_{\perp}-S^{\dagger}_{X}(\Psi_{\perp})\,.

(3.8)

Relative to an adapted frame, the components of the shape operators are given by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\mu}}}}{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\mu}}}}{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\mu}}}}{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c \displaystyle=\mathchoice{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\sigma^{{{I}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[(\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}P)\mathchoice{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{A}}}}{s^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}} .

, which shows that the three-index “
N
-beins” of Refs.
[
120
,
22
,
121
]
and the “quantum Christoffel symbols of the first kind” introduced in Ref.
[
122
]
can be interpreted as frame components of the second fundamental form
\underline{S}
(the latter quantity should not be confused with the Christoffel symbols or connection coefficients derived from the quantum metric described in
Section

3.3
below
[
120
,
36
]
).

In the current geometric setup, the covariant derivative of the shape operator is undefined.
This is because the index
\mu
in the expressions
\mathchoice{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\mu}}}}{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\mu}}}}{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\mu}}}}{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscr and
\mathchoice{S^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}{\mu}}}}{S^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}{\mu}}}}{S^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}{\mu}}}}{S^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}{\mu}}}}
refers to
TM
, the tangent bundle of
M
, and the only derivatives considered so far are defined in
E
and its sub-bundles
E_{\parallel}
and
E_{\perp}
.
To differentiate such objects, a covariant derivative
{\mathring{\nabla}}
is required on the base manifold
M
. Then, we can define a covariant derivative on the tensor products of
E_{\parallel}
,
E_{\perp}
, and
TM
(as well as their duals) via the rule

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{X}(\Phi_{\parallel}\otimes\Psi_{\perp}\otimes Y)=(D_{X}\Phi_{\parallel})\otimes\Psi_{\perp}\otimes Y+\Phi_{\parallel}\otimes(D^{\prime}_{X}\Psi_{\perp})\otimes Y+\Phi_{\parallel}\otimes\Psi_{\perp}\otimes({\mathring{\nabla}}_{X}Y)\,.

(3.11)

For example, for the shape operator
S
one then has

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{X}[S(\Phi_{\parallel},Y)]=({\mathscr{D}}_{X}S)(\Phi_{\parallel},Y)+S(D_{X}\Phi_{\parallel},Y)+S(\Phi_{\parallel},{\mathring{\nabla}}_{X}Y)\,.

(3.12)

The following analysis will make use of such a derivative
{\mathring{\nabla}}
for intermediate calculations – the final results, however, will be independent of this connection.

3.2
Berry curvature and the Gauss–Codazzi–Mainardi equations

Since the Berry connection is derived from the covariant derivative
\nabla
and the projection
P
, the Berry curvature of the projected connection can be expressed in terms of the projection of the curvature
\Omega
and the shape operators
S
and
S^{\dagger}
.
The precise relation is given by generalizations of the Gauss equation known from Riemannian (or pseudo-Riemannian) geometry. We also derive generalizations of the Codazzi–Mainardi equations that relate the covariant derivatives of the shape operators to the “mixed components” of
\Omega
with respect to the projections
P
and
P^{\prime}
. A similar generalization of the Gauss–Codazzi–Mainardi equations can also be found in Refs.
[
73
,
74
]
, although only for the case of holomorphic vector bundles over complex manifolds. In Ref.
[
32
]
, submanifold geometry and the Gauss–Codazzi–Mainardi equations are also used in connection with the quantum geometric tensor, but in a different way compared to our current treatment and for the specific case of submanifolds in
\mathbb{C}P^{n}
.

3.2.1
Gauss equations

The Gauss equations show that curvatures
\Omega^{\parallel}
(of
D
) and
\Omega^{\perp}
(of
D^{\prime}
) are fully determined by the curvature
\Omega
and the shape operators
S
and
S^{\dagger}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}h[\Phi_{\parallel},\Omega^{\parallel}(X,Y)\Psi_{\parallel}]&=h[\Phi_{\parallel},\Omega(X,Y)\Psi_{\parallel}]\\
&\qquad+h[S(\Phi_{\parallel},X),S(\Psi_{\parallel},Y)]-h[S(\Phi_{\parallel},Y),S(\Psi_{\parallel},X)]\,,\end{split}

(3.13a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}h[\Phi_{\perp},\Omega^{\perp}(X,Y)\Psi_{\perp}]&=h[\Phi_{\perp},\Omega(X,Y)\Psi_{\perp}]\\
&\qquad+h[S^{\dagger}(\Phi_{\perp},X),S^{\dagger}(\Psi_{\perp},Y)]-h[S^{\dagger}(\Phi_{\perp},Y),S^{\dagger}(\Psi_{\perp},X)]\,.\end{split}

(3.13b)

The first equation determines the Berry curvature
\mathscr{F}=\mathrm{i}\Omega^{\parallel}
in terms of
\Omega
and the shape operator, while the second equation represents a generalization of the Ricci equation for submanifolds
[
76
,
77
]
. In particular, note that the dependence of the Berry curvature on
\Omega
is a direct consequence of the non-flat connection
\nabla
that we assumed, in contrast to other works
[
28
,
36
,
33
]
which start with a flat connection. Working in this more general setting is essential for certain applications, such as semiclassical and high-frequency approximations for wave packets propagating on curved spacetime, where such additional terms arise in the Berry curvature
[
84
,
85
,
88
,
92
]
.

The Gauss equations can be derived as follows.
Differentiating
D_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel}=P\nabla_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel}
along
X
, one obtains, using
Eq.

3.8
,

\displaystyle D_{X}D_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel}=P(\nabla_{X}\nabla_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel})+P(\nabla_{X}P)S_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel}+P(\nabla_{X}P)D_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel}\,,

(3.14)

where we have used the notation
S_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel}=S(\Psi_{\parallel},Y)
introduced above.
Now, the last term vanishes since
D_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel}
takes values in
E_{\parallel}
, causing
(\nabla_{X}P)D_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel}
to take values in
E_{\perp}
, which is precisely the kernel of
P
.
In the second term,
S_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel}
takes values in
E_{\perp}
, so
P(\nabla_{X}P)
can be replaced by
S^{\dagger}_{X}
there.
This implies

\displaystyle h(\Phi_{\parallel},D_{X}D_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel})=h(\Phi_{\parallel},\nabla_{X}\nabla_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel})+h(S_{X}\Phi_{\parallel},S_{Y}\Psi_{\parallel})\,.

(3.15)

Anti-symmetrizing over
X
and
Y
, and using
h(\Phi_{\parallel},D_{[X,Y]}\Psi_{\parallel})=h(\Phi_{\parallel},\nabla_{[X,Y]}\Psi_{\parallel})
, one obtains
Eq.

3.13a
using the Ricci identity (
2.6
).
The derivation of
Eq.

3.13b
is completely analogous: one replaces
\parallel
by
\perp
and
P
by
P^{\prime}
; the difference in signs in
Eq.

3.8
plays no role since
S^{\dagger}
enters the final equation quadratically.

In index notation,
Eqs.

3.13a
and
3.13b
take the form

(3.16b)

3.2.2
Codazzi–Mainardi equations

As can be seen above, the Gauss equations (
3.16a
) and (
3.16b
) allow computing the projections of
\Omega(X,Y)
onto
E_{\parallel}
and
E_{\perp}
. Similarly, the Codazzi–Mainardi equations express the “mixed components”
\mathchoice{\Omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{\Omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{\Omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}{\Omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}}}}(X,Y)
and
\mathchoice{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}}}}{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}}}}{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}}}}{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}}}}(X,Y)
in terms of “exterior covariant derivatives” of the shape operators:

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{X}S(\Phi_{\parallel},Y)-{\mathscr{D}}_{Y}S(\Phi_{\parallel},X)

\displaystyle=+P^{\prime}[\Omega(X,Y)\Phi_{\parallel}]\,,

(3.17a)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{X}S^{\dagger}(\Psi_{\perp},Y)-{\mathscr{D}}_{Y}S^{\dagger}(\Psi_{\perp},X)

\displaystyle=-P[\Omega(X,Y)\Psi_{\perp}]\,,

(3.17b)

where
{\mathscr{D}}
is defined by means of
Eq.

3.12
, in which
{\mathring{\nabla}}
is
any
torsion-free connection on the base manifold
M
. These equations can be derived as follows.

By definition (
3.6a
), one has
S(\Phi_{\parallel},Y)=(\nabla_{Y}P)\Phi_{\parallel}=P^{\prime}\nabla_{Y}\Phi_{\parallel}
. Differentiating along
X
and using
Eq.

3.8
, one obtains

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}[S(\Phi_{\parallel},Y)]

\displaystyle=P^{\prime}[\nabla_{X}\nabla_{Y}\Phi_{\parallel}]-(\nabla_{X}P)D_{Y}\Phi_{\parallel}-(\nabla_{X}P)S(\Phi_{\parallel},Y)\,.

(3.18)

Applying
P^{\prime}
removes the last term, as
S(\Phi_{\parallel},Y)
is a section of
E_{\perp}
and is thus mapped by
\nabla_{X}P
to a section of
E_{\parallel}=\kernel P^{\prime}
. Hence, one finds

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{X}[S(\Phi_{\parallel},Y)]=P^{\prime}[\nabla_{X}\nabla_{Y}\Phi_{\parallel}]-S(D_{Y}\Phi_{\parallel},X)\,.

(3.19)

From
Eq.

3.12
one then obtains

\displaystyle\begin{split}[{\mathscr{D}}_{X}S](\Phi_{\parallel},Y)&=P^{\prime}[\nabla_{X}\nabla_{Y}\Phi_{\parallel}]-S(\Phi_{\parallel},{\mathring{\nabla}}_{X}Y)-S(D_{X}\Phi_{\parallel},Y)-S(D_{Y}\Phi_{\parallel},X)\\
&=P^{\prime}[\nabla_{X}\nabla_{Y}\Phi_{\parallel}-\nabla_{{\mathring{\nabla}}_{X}Y}\Phi_{\parallel}]-[S(D_{X}\Phi_{\parallel},Y)+S(D_{Y}\Phi_{\parallel},X)]\,.\end{split}

(3.20)

Anti-symmetrizing over
X
and
Y
, the last two terms cancel.
Moreover, since
{\mathring{\nabla}}
is assumed to be torsion-free, one has
{\mathring{\nabla}}_{X}Y-{\mathring{\nabla}}_{Y}X=[X,Y]
, where, as above,
[\cdot,\cdot]
denotes the Lie bracket of vector fields.
Equation

3.17a
is then obtained using the Ricci identity (
2.6
).
The proof of
Eq.

3.17b
is analogous and is therefore omitted.

In index notation,
Eqs.

3.17a
and
3.17b
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\nu}}}}{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\nu}}}}{S^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\ \displaystyle=+\mathchoice{\Omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\Omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A}{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\Omega^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.728 (3.21a)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{\mathscr{D}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{S^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}{\nu}}}}{S^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{I}{\nu}}}}{S^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\
